## R9 candidate grammar/profile premises (N9)

PROPOSED — EV-118–121 transcribe the reviewed A4 R2 seam conditionally. P_A4 is A4's one recommended candidate profile, used only to render this proposal and its finite checks. No exact word, keyword form or spelling alternative is selected by this draft. All spellings, members/elements and the set words remain the explicit alternatives in E-40's open choice register and OPEN_CHOICES.md. SOURCE — O1 bound two is answered A (0 then1). [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-loop-o1-a-and-arch-fixes.json; E-22/O1 A, bound two only] PROPOSED — General positive enumeration and loop charges remain open. Changing a profile requires a newly fixed inventory and fresh source/name/gate checks; alternatives are not aliases in P_A4. [R9_A4:167–181,185–209,304–323,333; R9_F0264:31]

PROPOSED — The following is the conditional G(P_A4) transcription. Token productions and concrete delimiters below describe that candidate only. Each production cites R9_A4's exact R2 lines 70–90, in the same order; no author parser is imported or executed. Semantic metanotation elsewhere in the inherited rules is not silently turned into source text. A broader complete presentation/admission implementation and a general grammar/unambiguity proof remain open.

```bnf
program  ::= decl*
decl     ::= NAME 'has' NAT 'cases'                                   -- a choice, as D-VAL-2 writes it
           | ['memo'] 'def' NAME '(' [NAME {',' NAME}] ')' '=' expr  -- parameters are binders
expr     ::= 'let' NAME '=' expr ';' expr
           | 'for' NAME '<' expr 'from' NAME '=' expr 'do' expr
           | 'case' expr 'of' REF '{' [branches] '}'
           | atom
branches ::= branch {';' branch} [';' 'else' '=>' expr] | 'else' '=>' expr
branch   ::= NAT '(' NAME ')' '=>' expr
atom     ::= NAT | BYTES | 'set' '{' [lit {',' lit}] '}'
           | 'row' '(' [expr {',' expr}] ')'
           | REF '.' NAT '(' expr ')'                                 -- a case of a choice
           | REF '(' [expr {',' expr}] ')'                            -- a primitive or a definition
           | REF                                                      -- a use of a variable
           | '(' expr ')'
lit      ::= NAT | BYTES | 'row' '(' [lit {',' lit}] ')' | REF '.' NAT '(' lit ')' | 'set' '{' [lit {',' lit}] '}'
NAT      ::= 0 | a nonzero decimal digit followed by decimal digits
BYTES    ::= x"" or x" followed by pairs of lower-case hex digits, then "
IDENT    ::= a lower-case ASCII letter, then lower-case letters, digits or _ ; not a keyword
NAME     ::= an IDENT that is not a reserved name (2.2)               -- declarations and binders only
REF      ::= an IDENT                                                 -- every reference; resolved by 2.2
```

PROPOSED — For P_A4 the keywords are `def`, `memo`, `has`, `cases`, `let`, `case`, `of`, `else`, `for`, `from`, `do`, `row`, `set`. IDENT excludes these; NAME further excludes the 26 candidate active primitive labels, the three built-in choices and the one held word read. REF permits reserved identifiers in reference positions and is then resolved by role. Source identifiers are ASCII under this candidate and need no normalization; this adds no external-choice identity, canonical byte format or count/number codec. [R9_A4:88–90,117–130]

PROPOSED — P_A4's conditional active inventory is exactly the following. Primitive counts are argument arities; order/truth/option counts are choice-case counts. Listing a role transcribes the existing proposal's inventory, not a new operation equation, bytes contract, identity law, tariff or signature choice. Held words are reserved and uninstalled; recommendations and alternatives outside this inventory install nothing. SOURCE — Slice takes start and length. ## R8 four-natural-operation premises (N8)

PROPOSED — Conditional on the reviewed EV-A1 proposal as repaired by R8_Q F1–F9, EV-114–117 are four DRAFT contracts. Both operands must be admitted arbitrary finite naturals; there is no cap, negative natural or host-width restriction. Write a=ell(x), b=ell(y), m=max(a,b), with ell(0)=1. For valid natural operands, each invoice depends on a and b alone, not equality, zero, scan length or host method. Admitted wrong-kind operands instead use Rule E with the parts actually supplied (EV-086/092): a natural's part is ell, bytes have length+1, and another admitted value has part 1. Malformed values remain admission refusals. [R8_A1:34–41; R8_Q:15–24,32 (F1)]

For a valid invocation, A/R/T and the complete known W are prepaid once as one invoice before computing or constructing the result. On insufficient remaining operation fuel, this primitive takes no fuel and builds or changes nothing; earlier evaluation is not undone. Equal-length compare pays ell(first) even when its actual scan stops sooner; no second debit is charged for those same events. Separately specified dynamic set-search events retain their own protocol. [R8_METER:40,44; R8_Q:20–21,34 (F3)]

PROPOSED — Compute nonnegative invoice arithmetic exactly, or compare it safely with remaining fuel before constructing the result. Lengths and their products are uncapped; wrapped arithmetic and partial debit are forbidden. A shadow-budget method may compare each nonnegative term before subtraction, guard a positive product u*v by u > remaining//v before multiplying, and commit only after every term fits. This is a mathematical method, not evidence about a native implementation. [R8_Q:33 (F2); EV-019]

PROPOSED — R is the full prospective bound, charged even for zero, a shorter answer or canonical reuse, with no refund. T=0 here is the proposed designated-frame tariff, not a physical-workspace assertion. A for naturals and these natural-result R bounds count bit cells; T counts frames; W follows each entry, including bit-position pairs for mul. Unit calibration remains open in E-3. A different unit requires revised entries and thresholds; dividing and rounding lengths is not a conversion theorem. Invoice arithmetic itself has no additional recursive tariff in this proposal. [R8_A1:38–39,119–124; R8_Q:22,28,38 (F7)]

## Shared K-14 invariant and finite fixture F

PROPOSED — Shared rule premises. Write M(s) for a valid finite set's mathematical member collection. Validity requires each member to be an admitted evaluator value, no repeated member, canonical ordering, and the same canonical set identity for equal member collections. The member domain includes every admitted evaluator value: a sequence identity itself, and an identity at every finite immutable nesting depth through rows, case payloads and nested sets. Sequence equality/order uses its supplied stable identity; it never consults current contents. An immutable containing value and its set therefore retain identity, membership and order when a contained sequence's state changes. These are required invariants of every admitted set arriving by a permitted literal, input, reachable starting-state value or set construction. They do not specify how admission visits those sources, what literals are permitted, which state roots exist, or how identities are supplied or encoded. An admitted non-set first operand gives bare none under EV-084. A malformed or otherwise unadmitted value is not made admitted by Q3 and retains its separate admission boundary. Canonical validity does not supply the executable visiting procedure or complete K-13 charge.

PROPOSED — Define the following finite immutable values and canonical sets in dependency order: w0=a and S0={a}; w1=Row(a) and S1={Row(a)}; w2=Case(chi,0,a) and S2={w2}; w3=S0 and S3={S0}; w4=Row(Case(chi,0,S1)) and S4={w4}. These exercise a direct sequence member, row, case, nested set, and a row→case→set→row→sequence path of depth four. The rule's all-depth domain is the delegated decision, not a conclusion proved by these five witnesses. Let E be the canonical empty set. For each j in the exact finite index set {0,1,2,3,4}, let Uj be the canonical set {wj,b}. Under the supplied K-3 order, wj < b for all five j: a < b for j=0, and the row/case/set kinds precede the sequence kind for j=1–4. Thus members(Sj)=Row(wj) and members(Uj)=Row(wj,b), with no unspecified sort permutation. Braces are mathematical set notation, not a reader syntax or sequence literal.

Equal-set identity below means the same canonical set, including an exactly unchanged set on duplicate insert/absent remove; it is stronger than merely equal cardinality or equal printed members. The cases are exact author-drafted semantic witnesses, not RAN checks. If integrated separately, retain F and these premises or expand them in the receiving rule file.

- text: A sequence is a finite order of containers holding realized evaluator values. It is an ordinary value with identity; its current held values can change while it stays the same sequence. The proposed six-kind operation inventory is not adopted by this definition.

## EV-002 — Identity-preserving sequence change

- revision: 3
- standing: working — only R3_STAY's adopted identity-preserving change decision; exact primitive return, trail format and costs remain draft/open.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:37–48 (EV-002, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:44–55 (EV-002); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json.
- text: Changing a held value changes the sequence itself while it remains the same sequence. Every holder of that sequence still holds that same changed sequence. The change is a fact whether or not it is subsequently observed.
- case: Let a and b denote the same sequence s, holding 4 at supplied valid selector i. A permitted completed replacement of that held value by 9 leaves a and b denoting the same s, now holding 9 there. Identity is unchanged. This is a state observation, requiring no chosen direct position-read API or numeric selector. The fact of this change also holds on a path with no subsequent observation.

## EV-003 — Equal values are one node

- revision: 5
- standing: draft — inherited binding standing as recorded in the pinned R3 OLD_RULES; new details are draft and numeric calibration remains open.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:49–60 (EV-003, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:56–67 (EV-003); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json. Current R6 case adjudication: R6_FUEL_INVENTORY; R6_METER:36–50,58–104; R6_PROCESS_RECORD, authenticated R6_PROCESS_LINES; FUEL_CLAUSES.md. R14 sources: owner-record-2026-11-11-web-oq1-oq3-a.json#OQ1, DIGEST.json#F002, DIGEST.json#purityfresh.
- text: Equal admitted immutable evaluator values are one observable node under the candidate canonical representation; equality of a contained sequence means equality of its atomic handle, not equality of current contents. Equal sets also require canonical distinct sorted members and equal-set interning. EV-039 supplies only the conditional order premises. SOURCE — Computing a term once and reusing its result is permitted only when the term makes nothing new, has no effect, has no open choice, consumes nothing use-once, and has a result that may be copied. State-freedom alone is insufficient. [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-web-oq1-oq3-a.json; OQ1 A with Arch condition] Equal code or an equal represented node does not itself identify distinct executions or discharge the duty to make each required fresh result, including a result later unused. Immutable-value canonicalization is not a licence to omit or merge fresh production. [digest: DIGEST.json; F002, purityfresh]
- case: Construct admitted row(4,7) by two construction paths. Under the canonical-node and EV-041 built-in premises, same(a,b) yields Case(truth,1,row()). Two distinct admitted sequence handles with equal current contents are not equated by this example. SOURCE — Two executions of make(1,0) produce distinct sequences; interning the code does not make a write through one alter the other. OQ1 also preserves separate fresh use-once results; their dynamic-name representation and matching checks remain E-21. [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-web-oq1-oq3-a.json; OQ1 A with Arch condition]
- excludes: Case(truth,0,row()) merely because the equal rows were reached separately, or merging distinct sequence handles from equal current contents.
- gaps: Specific inherited boundary: E-14/E-16/E-23 retain physical interning, bytes and complete identity supply; E-3/E-12 charges. Q3 is decided Yes by verified delegation (EV-056). No mutable contents traversal belongs to immutable equality/order. Current policy: strict copy-then-row reading, fixed length, every-admitted-value all-depth set domain, start-derived logical scope, no whole-sequence write, invalid memo-mark refusal, position-size write charging with no sequence marker [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json] and zero-origin positions. The surviving edge results EV-070–079/082–084 and case16 B at EV-038/040 are decided; none of these is a current Q1/C8 owner wait. EV-062/063/064 now draft K-1/K-4/K-12 cores under their explicit premises. Technical work remains E-3/E-12/E-27 complete metering, E-4/E-11/E-16 complete syntax/admission/bytes, E-18 interfaces and E-23/E-25/E-26 executable identity/state/evidence integration. Direct reading is a later option E-29, reconsider-A is E-39 and exact names are E-40. No complete contract adoption is inferred. ## EV-004 — Identity result carrier

- revision: 3
- standing: draft — PROPOSED K-8 truth carrier attached to the retained same-identity law, under supplied identity/interning premises.
- kind: definition
- subject: an evaluator build on admitted values with full EV-041 binding/payload premises and the applicable stable identity/canonical-node premises
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:61–72 (EV-004, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:68–79 (EV-004); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. - text: PROPOSED `same` returns primitive-produced `Case(truth,1,Row())` exactly when its two operands are one node, and `Case(truth,0,Row())` otherwise. For sequence handles, the premise is equality of the supplied stable sequence identities, not equality of current contents. For immutable values, coherent equal-node interning is an explicit obligation. This result law selects no K-1 scope/hash/supply recipe and supplies no physical interning implementation.
- case: PROPOSED Supply distinct sequence identities `alpha != beta` for admitted handles `s` and `t`, both holding the single value `0`. `P(x)=Row(same(s,s),same(s,t))` on input `0` successfully yields `Row(Case(truth,1,Row()),Case(truth,0,Row()))`. An alias of `s` replaces either occurrence of `s` without changing these results. A valid mutation of what `s` holds likewise leaves the identity observations unchanged. This fixture supplies its identities; it does not construct them.
- excludes: PROPOSED A natural `1`/`0` carrier, truth cases with nonempty primitive-produced payloads, yes for distinct supplied `alpha` and `beta` merely because both contents are `0`, or no for two occurrences of `s` after its contents change.
- gaps: EV-038/040/041 draft the reference and primitive/user payload distinctions. EV-062 drafts logical identity/supply under explicit bytes/hash/collision premises; full validation and canonical-value bytes remain E-14/E-16/E-23, with all charges E-3/E-12/E-27. This identity case supplies no executable codec. - subject: an evaluator build on admitted natural operands with the full EV-041 binding/payload premises
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:133–144 (EV-010, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:140–151 (EV-010); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json. Current R6 case adjudication: R6_FUEL_INVENTORY; R6_METER:36–50,58–104; R6_PROCESS_RECORD, authenticated R6_PROCESS_LINES; FUEL_CLAUSES.md. Current R8: R8_A1:57–59,91,102; R8_Q:19,32–38,48; EV-117.
- text: PROPOSED Public `compare` has naturals as its valid ordinary operand domain and returns a case of the completely bound built-in `order/3`, with its primitive-result empty-row payload. The internal K-3 order does not thereby become public comparison for bytes, rows, cases, sets or sequence handles. This domain statement supplies no wrong-shaped-argument result or refusal policy. Conditional on EV-117, compare(x,y) returns exactly Case((order,3),0,Row()) for x<y, Case((order,3),1,Row()) for x=y, and Case((order,3),2,Row()) for x>y.
- case: Conditional on EV-117's DRAFT equation, P(x)=compare(1,2) on input 0, if the full run completes, outputs exactly Case((order,3),0,Row()). With operands already obtained its proposed primitive A/R/T/W is 3/0/0/1, total 4: remaining operation fuel 4 completes, while 3 stops unchanged with no primitive result. No sufficient complete-program fuel is supplied.
- excludes: PROPOSED An unwrapped natural `1`, a case of truth/2 or another choice, an ordinal outside `0..2`, or a nonempty payload as this valid-domain primitive result. This record draws no outcome conclusion for `compare(B[],B[])` or any other operand outside the stated public domain.
- gaps: The natural equation and primitive invoice are now DRAFT in EV-117; wrong-kind values still use EV-084/092. Other equations, syntax, canonical bytes and complete-program charges remain E-18/E-16/E-27. No public heterogeneous comparison is supplied. Current policy: strict copy-then-row reading, fixed length, every-admitted-value all-depth set domain, start-derived logical scope, no whole-sequence write, invalid memo-mark refusal, position-size write charging with no sequence marker [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json] and zero-origin positions. ## EV-011 — Quotient/remainder row carrier

- revision: 4
- standing: draft — reviewed conditional divmod equation/invoice proposal; the inherited row carrier remains; no adoption of positive equation or tariff.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build executing divmod on already obtained admitted natural operands, with d above zero, under N15
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:145–156 (EV-011, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:152–163 (EV-011); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json. Current R6 case adjudication: R6_FUEL_INVENTORY; R6_METER:36–50,58–104; R6_PROCESS_RECORD, authenticated R6_PROCESS_LINES; FUEL_CLAUSES.md. R15: SOURCE EV_A6:40–244; G_RECHECK:13–26; OWNER_RECORD/OWNER_RAW (set choice only); N15. Exact alias pins are in the current INPUT_BINDINGS.json.
- text: PROPOSED For admitted naturals x,d with d>0, divmod(x,d)=Row(q,r), the unique natural pair satisfying x=q*d+r and 0<=r<d. The immutable two-field row has quotient first and remainder second, not a case wrapper or mutable sequence. Let a=ell(x), b=ell(d), Rq=max(1,a-b+1), Rr=min(a,b). Under N15 use (A,R,T,W)=(a+b,Rq+Rr+3,0,Rq*b). Rq bounds the quotient, Rr the remainder and 3 the two-field row; W counts prospective quotient-position/divisor-position pairs, regardless of implementation speed. Pay all components before constructing the quotient, remainder or row; all are built or none. Wrong kind is classified first under Rule E; d=0 instead uses EV-071. This is the missing proposed universal equation, not something forced by the old (7,3) calibration.
- case: PROPOSED Obtained-operand cases, with result; A/R/T/W; total: D01 divmod(7,3)=Row(2,1);5/7/0/4;16. D02 divmod(0,5)=Row(0,0);4/5/0/3;12. D03 divmod(5,7)=Row(0,5);6/7/0/3;16. D04 divmod(8,1)=Row(8,0);5/8/0/4;17. D05 divmod(7,7)=Row(1,0);6/7/0/3;16. D06 divmod(2^64,2^32)=Row(2^32,0);98/69/0/1089;1256. D07 divmod(2^128-1,2^64-1)=Row(2^64+1,0);192/132/0/4160;4484. D08 divmod(2^64-1,2^64)=Row(0,2^64-1);129/68/0/65;262. D11 divmod(Row(),3)=none;3/0/0/1;4 by actual-part Rule E. Every threshold has N15's one-less stop control. D09/D10 are in EV-071; these are operation-stage cases, not whole-program budgets.
- excludes: PROPOSED Rounding the quotient other than down, a positive-divisor remainder outside 0<=r<d, swapped fields, a case/sequence carrier, a discount for zero/short/existing results, machine-word W, second payment for the same work, overflowed invoices or construction before full payment. This rule does not apply the positive remainder bound at d=0 or assign zero's tariff; EV-071 does that separately.
- gaps: PROPOSED R15 supplies the positive-divisor equation/invoice and wrong-kind operation-stage calibration conditionally; EV-071 separately supplies the zero result and proposed zero tariff. E-2/E-18 are narrowed, not closed. Row admission/identity/bytes (E-4/E-14/E-16), complete integration and meter assurance (E-3/E-12/E-27), unit/work bounds, positive T and unavailable-bound witnesses remain open. Inherited R6/R10 accounting below is historical and is narrowed only by the R15 entries above. R6: EV-086–113 now draft the listed K-13 operation table and E/L (former Rule S retired) [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json] after bounded G0172 review; this does not complete or adopt the whole meter. R10 drafts only the EV-122–125 structural/no-turn/entry/admission-boundary/unit proposal. Remaining natural and byte entries, positive-loop setup/turn A/R/T/counter charges, separate admission resources, complete meter integration, EV-018/021 witnesses, exact spellings and any host calibration outside semantic units remain open. R10 N10 supplies the conditional fresh completed-unary EV-024/026 witnesses; incomplete/warmed/resumed/restoration and general integration remain open. - standing: draft — inherited binding standing as recorded in the pinned R3 OLD_RULES; new details are draft and numeric calibration remains open.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:301–312 (EV-024, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:308–319 (EV-024); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. - text: For completed calls eligible under the proposed conservative transitive state-free gate EV-047, memoization keys a completed unary call by its argument's evaluator identity. SOURCE — Computing a term once and reusing its result is permitted only when the term makes nothing new, has no effect, has no open choice, consumes nothing use-once, and has a result that may be copied. State-freedom alone is insufficient. [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-web-oq1-oq3-a.json; OQ1 A with Arch condition] SOURCE — A memo call reaching its own unfinished key has no completed hit and runs under ordinary fuel; it does not reuse an unfinished result or stop early as unknown solely because that key is unfinished. [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-web-oq1-oq3-a.json; OQ3 A] The state-free predicate and memo-mark refusal rule are unchanged; the placement and proof of the additional reuse obligations remain E-13. No hit/miss tariff is selected by these decisions. SOURCE — Under Claude A's recorded M-C call, made under the owner's delegation, memo marks are permitted only on one-argument definitions. Ordinary several-argument calls continue. An author who wants such computation memoized passes one row explicitly to a one-argument definition; the evaluator does not automatically pack several arguments. Zero-argument memo waits and is outside this M-C profile. [SOURCE: owner-record-2026-11-12-memo-key-delegated-mc.json, authorizes_only; DECISIONS.postimage.md:39,51; BRIEF-MEMO-KEY.md:18,28,32-35.] PROPOSED — Scope the inherited unary identity key to the called definition and its single evaluated argument, so g(4) cannot reuse h(4)'s answer. The concrete representation of definition identity remains E-13. [SOURCE: owner-record-2026-11-12-memo-key-delegated-mc.json, authorizes_only; DECISIONS.postimage.md:39,51; BRIEF-MEMO-KEY.md:18,28,32-35.]
- case: N10 W24, under EV-122–125 and eligible completed unary fresh-memo premises: memo g(y)=row(y,y); f(x)=let a=g(row(x)); g(row(x)), input4. Strict evaluation forces the first fill. The separately constructed immutable row(4) arguments have the same inherited evaluator identity. Identity-keyed cost25 returns row(row(4),row(4)); occurrence-keyed cost34. At fuel25 only the correct build returns, while the fault stops at the second miss. Across32 sensitivity settings the targeted fault spends5 through9 more; this is a conditional supplied witness, not complete memo/identity implementation.
- excludes: The occurrence-keyed second call misses solely because the equal argument arose at a different occurrence. W24's distinct completion observation at the stated conditional budget rejects that fault. Do not claim a new canonical identity law, incomplete-entry result or unconditional tariff.
- gaps: N10 now supplies the specifically named conditional fresh completed-unary miss/hit and cache-lifetime observations; broader cache/evidence behavior remains open. E-13/E-19/E-20 retain keys, incomplete entries, lifetime and call/hit/miss composition; EV-064 supplies only a fresh count-zero empty-memo record. R6: EV-086–113 now draft the listed K-13 operation table and E/L (former Rule S retired) [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json] after bounded G0172 review; this does not complete or adopt the whole meter. - text: For completed calls eligible under the proposed conservative transitive state-free gate EV-047, the memo cache lives for the whole run. The cache has run lifetime; returning to a kept point restores its saved entries. SOURCE — Computing a term once and reusing its result is permitted only when the term makes nothing new, has no effect, has no open choice, consumes nothing use-once, and has a result that may be copied. State-freedom alone is insufficient. [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-web-oq1-oq3-a.json; OQ1 A with Arch condition] SOURCE — A memo call reaching its own unfinished key has no completed hit and runs under ordinary fuel; it does not reuse an unfinished result or stop early as unknown solely because that key is unfinished. [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-web-oq1-oq3-a.json; OQ3 A] The state-free predicate and memo-mark refusal rule are unchanged; the placement and proof of the additional reuse obligations remain E-13. No hit/miss tariff is selected by these decisions. PROPOSED — Install only completed results satisfying the unchanged reuse premises; interrupted work supplies no reusable partial answer. Preserve run-long completed entries without unannounced eviction and restore the saved entries at a kept point. Reservation is distinct from installing a completed answer. Retained arguments, results and saved cache states require the E-13/E-20 resource account; interrupted, warmed, resumed and restoration details and charges remain open. No memory saving or equal-fuel claim follows from M-C. [SOURCE: owner-record-2026-11-12-memo-key-delegated-mc.json, authorizes_only; DECISIONS.postimage.md:39,51; BRIEF-MEMO-KEY.md:18,28,32-35.]

## EV-034 — Separate scoped soundness

- revision: 4
- standing: draft — inherited binding standing as recorded in the pinned R3 OLD_RULES; new details are draft and numeric calibration remains open.
- kind: requirement
- subject: a build using a checking run to settle its input statement
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:421–432 (EV-034, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:428–439 (EV-034); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current R6 case adjudication: R6_FUEL_INVENTORY; R6_METER:36–50,58–104; R6_PROCESS_RECORD, authenticated R6_PROCESS_LINES; FUEL_CLAUSES.md.
- text: Passing from a checker's affirmative result to its input statement requires the separate soundness claim for that statement form, theory and scope.
- case: Supply a checker accept(x)=Case(truth,1,row()) under EV-041 binding/payload premises. On a supplied statement value v denoting 0=1, a sufficiently fueled run yields that affirmative value. Without the separate soundness claim for this statement form, theory and scope, 0=1 does not follow.

## EV-038 — Complete choice identity, case range and one payload

- revision: 4
- standing: DRAFT — reviewed K-2 reference core carried forward with the verified case16 option-B decision; full admission traversal and presentation/encoding remain open. This is author drafting, not independent acceptance.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build for a program with unique local choice bindings and admitted case values, subject to the admission and encoding limits stated below
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:469–480 (EV-038, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:476–486 carries the complete-identity/order/one-payload and reference-validity core with its inherited review provenance. ARCH:168,177 specifies option B's K-2 point-2 change. CASE16 /owner_line,/authorizes_only and its raw human line SHA256 `3147a6f20a93f48189a84bc7a30997f81f194a45341d4d46c5c104ec39150031` bind the owner's later B choice and reconsider-A requirement; see OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json. R12: A5_R2:54-170,174-289,424-428; G0181:9-14,37-39; C_ID:1,9,137-149; card0470. R15 SOURCE — METHOD:70 and ARCH_REPAIRS:70–78 (F005). PROPOSED — Clause markings distinguish existing denotations/duties/notes only; no behavior is added or removed.
- text: [definition] A choice has complete identity `(name,k)`, with natural `k`, including zero. [definition] Equal names and equal `k` identify the same choice; identity order is name length, name bytes within equal lengths, then `k`. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build admitting declarations and case values, resolving references or running case analysis under the stated premises] A program's user declarations have pairwise different names: equal-name declarations are refused even when their counts agree. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build admitting declarations and case values, resolving references or running case analysis under the stated premises] The user names `order`, `truth` and `option` are reserved; [definition] their implicit program bindings are `order/3`, `truth/2` and `option/2`. [definition] A local constructor or branch spelling denotes the one choice bound to that name. [definition] A case has a natural ordinal `j` satisfying `0 <= j < k` and exactly one admitted evaluator-value payload; an empty row is one payload, not zero payloads. [definition] A zero-case choice has no case values. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build admitting declarations and case values, resolving references or running case analysis under the stated premises] An out-of-range constructor or branch ordinal, or a branch naming a choice other than its case expression's selected choice, is refused before the run. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build admitting declarations and case values, resolving references or running case analysis under the stated premises] An incoming malformed case is refused at the start against its own name, count and ordinal. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build admitting declarations and case values, resolving references or running case analysis under the stated premises] External values retain their full `(name,k)` identity: a local binding neither rewrites an external count nor bans an otherwise valid external identity merely because its name equals a builtin name. [definition] The reference environment is the implicit builtin bindings plus the unique user declarations; [requirement; subject: an evaluator build admitting declarations and case values, resolving references or running case analysis under the stated premises] unresolved constructor, selected-choice and branch references are refused. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build admitting declarations and case values, resolving references or running case analysis under the stated premises] Required case validity applies recursively to every permitted literal, input and reachable starting-state value. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build admitting declarations and case values, resolving references or running case analysis under the stated premises] In a case analysis, a subject that is not a case of the selected complete choice gives the bare builtin `none` and runs no branch, as detailed by EV-040; it does not enter `else`.
- note: [note; addressed to authors and reviewers] This statement does not supply the complete visiting procedure or byte decoder.
- case: Declare local `c/2`. `Case((c,2),1,Row(4,7))` has one two-field-row payload. A separately supplied valid external `Case((c,3),1,Row(4,7))` has a different identity and gives bare `none`, with no branch run, when analyzed against `c/2`. A local constructor at ordinal 2 is refused before the run; supplied input `Case((c,2),2,Row())` is refused at the start. Declaring `c/2` with `c/3`, or two `c/2` declarations, is refused before a name-only use can choose either. User declaration `truth/3` is refused, while valid external `Case((truth,3),1,5)` retains its own identity and gives bare `none` against target `truth/2`. Declaration `z/0` is permitted by the natural-count core but has no valid case or ordinal branch.
- note: [note; addressed to authors and reviewers] These are finite drafted witnesses, not executed Loom tests or numerical meter oracles.
- excludes: Choosing the first or last duplicate declaration; shadowing a builtin; equating external `c/3` with local `c/2`; admitting ordinal `k`; treating row fields as several case payloads; imposing primitive canonical-payload restrictions on generic builtin constructors; treating builtin-name reservation as a ban on valid external identities; skipping nested validity; or routing a wrong-choice or noncase subject to `else`.
- gaps: E-4/E-11/E-24 retain complete source and reference syntax, recursive traversal and validation placement over every permitted literal/input/reachable-starting-state value. E-4/E-16 retain the name alphabet/normalization and concrete bytes; E-14 retains canonical representation. E-18/E-19 retain branch binding/interfaces and broader order integration. E-3/E-12 retain complete admission, dispatch, construction, key and stopping costs. The explicit reconsider-A open item below must be retained before design and implementation doors close; B governs the current draft until changed by owner authority. Reconsideration item E-39; bounded grammar at CASE-GRAMMAR.md. ## EV-039 — Conditional internal structural order

- revision: 2
- standing: draft — PROPOSED conditional K-3 law; no completed K-1 identity contract, K-2 reference contract or meter.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build on admitted finite acyclic immutable values, treating sequence handles atomically
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:481–492 (EV-039, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:488–499 (EV-039); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json. R12: A5_R2:54-170,174-289,424-428; G0181:9-14,37-39; C_ID:1,9,137-149; card0470.
- text: PROPOSED Assume complete admitted choice identities have the K-2 order above and admitted sequence identities use the selected current-run-independent order of scope length, scope bytes, then creation count, respecting equality: comparison is zero exactly for equal identities. SOURCE — [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-oc-05-09-recs.json; OC-05 B; OC-06 Keep; OC-07 A; clarifies owner-record-2026-11-11-oc-03-05-08.json] PROPOSED — Complete identity/profile/admission premises remain required. Assume admitted rows, cases and sets are finite and acyclic as immutable structures; a sequence handle is atomic even if its mutable contents are cyclic. Assume sets have canonical sorted distinct member rows and that equal immutable values, including equal sets, are interned as one node. Under these premises, define the internal order by kind first: natural, bytes, row, case, set, sequence. Naturals follow magnitude; bytes follow length then byte order; rows follow length then their corresponding fields recursively; cases follow complete choice identity, case ordinal, then payload recursively; sets follow cardinality then their canonical member rows recursively; sequences follow the supplied identity order. A pair already known to be the same node compares equal; otherwise compare the specified components left to right and stop at the first difference. Set canonicalization must preserve this order, uniqueness and equal-set interning together. This is an internal mathematical order, not an extension of public `compare` to every kind.
- case: PROPOSED Internal comparison of `B[ff]` with `B[00,00]` gives less, as does `Row(9)` with `Row(0,0)`, because length precedes contents. Natural `9` precedes `B[]` by kind. With the K-2 fixture identities `chi < psi`, `Case(chi,1,5)` precedes `Case(psi,0,0)`, because choice precedes ordinal. The canonical member rows of supplied two-member sets `S{2,10}` and `T{3,4}` are `(2,10)` and `(3,4)`; the first set precedes the second. Supply distinct stable sequence keys `alpha < beta` for handles `s` and `t`, both presently holding `Row(0)`: `s < t` before and after a valid change to the contents of `s`. Two separately reached admitted equal sets with canonical members `(2,10)` must be one node. These are exact finite order/identity observations, not claims that a public comparison call accepts those operands.
- excludes: PROPOSED Byte-first comparison yielding `B[00,00] < B[ff]`; field-first comparison yielding `Row(0,0) < Row(9)`; ordinal-first comparison across different choices; sorting sets by their construction paths; equating `s` and `t` from equal current contents; changing their relative order after a content write; or retaining distinct observable nodes for equal canonical sets under the stated interning premise.
- gaps: The conditional internal structural order retains all original premises; EV-062 now drafts the identity/supply core under explicit canonical-byte/hash/collision premises. E-14/E-16 retain canonicalization/bytes; E-3/E-12/E-27 retain key/comparison/traversal temporary/work/debit duties. No physical interning or zero-temporary theorem follows. R6: EV-086–113 now draft the listed K-13 operation table and E/L (former Rule S retired) [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json] after bounded G0172 review; this does not complete or adopt the whole meter. R10 drafts only the EV-122–125 structural/no-turn/entry/admission-boundary/unit proposal. Remaining natural and byte entries, positive-loop setup/turn A/R/T/counter charges, separate admission resources, complete meter integration, EV-018/021 witnesses, exact spellings and any host calibration outside semantic units remain open. R10 N10 supplies the conditional fresh completed-unary EV-024/026 witnesses; incomplete/warmed/resumed/restoration and general integration remain open. The comparison-event/frame schedule is now explicitly bound by the new draft set-search comparison entry and its pinned SV3/S4/M6 sources; all other conditional premises and meter, admission, identity and integration gaps remain. SOURCE — OC-07 A selects the fixed scope-length/bytes/count order. [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-oc-05-09-recs.json; OC-05 B; OC-06 Keep; OC-07 A; clarifies owner-record-2026-11-11-oc-03-05-08.json] PROPOSED — P0 agreement still depends on its complete canonical profile and proof; no full codec or cross-profile admission follows. PROPOSED — Hash-profile replacement must preserve these laws within a compatible admitted profile; it does not prove correspondence between old and new names, set order, comparison charges or finite-fuel outcomes.

## EV-040 — Bound-choice dispatch, same-choice else and outside none

- revision: 3
- standing: DRAFT — K-2/K-7 evaluate-once, selected-only, range, duplicate and complete-identity core, revised by the verified owner choice of option B; full grammar, traversal, interfaces and costs remain open.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build for an admitted case expression with one fully bound selected choice and admitted ordinal branches
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:493–504 (EV-040, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:500–510 carries the earlier evaluate-once, selected-only and validity core with its inherited review provenance. ARCH:168,177 supplies option B and exhaustive else omission. CASE16 /owner_line,/authorizes_only is independently matched to raw human SHA256 `3147a6f20a93f48189a84bc7a30997f81f194a45341d4d46c5c104ec39150031`; its later B answer supersedes the earlier raw human A answer SHA256 `445982bc046acfed8b47f0a81c9971bfb8aac060135aeb4e305444205e1618a7`.
- text: A case expression selects one complete locally bound choice identity `chi=(name,k)`. Every ordinal branch must refer to `chi`, use an ordinal below `k` and be the only branch for that ordinal; mixed-choice, out-of-range and duplicate ordinal branches are refused before the run. There is at most one terminal `else`. An `else` is required unless the expression has an ordinal branch for every case of `chi`; when all are covered it may be present or omitted. Evaluate the subject once. If it is an admitted case of complete identity `chi` and a branch for its ordinal exists, evaluate only that ordinal branch body. If it is an admitted case of `chi` whose ordinal is uncovered, evaluate only `else`. If the subject is a valid case of any other complete choice, or is not a case at all, return the bare canonical builtin `none` and evaluate no branch body, including `else`, whether or not the target is exhaustively covered. This is a dispatch result after evaluating the subject: any effects of subject evaluation remain; no branch effect is added. Dispatch tests complete choice identity and ordinal, not equality with a primitive's canonical payload. Incoming case validity is an admission condition; a malformed case is refused at the start rather than normalized into an ordinary outside-choice subject.
- case: Target declared `c/3` with branches `0 -> 10`, `2 -> 30`, `else -> 90`. Subjects `Case((c,3),0,Row())`, `Case((c,3),1,Row())`, valid external `Case((c,4),0,Row())` and natural `7` give respectively `10,90,none,none`; the last two run no branch. Add `1 -> 20`: target ordinals now give `10,20,30`, while foreign and noncase subjects still give `none`; omitting `else` preserves those results. Omitting `else` before adding branch 1 is refused before the run. A second branch 0, ordinal-3 branch or branch naming declared `d/3` is refused before the run. Valid external `Case((c,4),3,Row())` gives `none`; malformed `Case((c,4),4,Row())` is refused at start. Under target `truth/2`, branch 1 takes both `Case((truth,2),1,Row())` and generic `Case((truth,2),1,5)`, passing their respective one payloads. Under an independently supplied valid-write premise, put a write in an unselected ordinal branch or in `else`: neither executes for a wrong-choice or noncase subject; an uncovered same-choice subject alone executes that `else`. These are proposed cases, not executed resource checks.
- excludes: Matching foreign `c/4` by name alone; sending a wrong-choice or noncase subject to `else`; wrapping the outside result as `some(none)`; running any branch for such a subject; requiring `else` after complete coverage; omitting it with an uncovered target ordinal; choosing a duplicate branch by order; running a mixed-choice or out-of-range branch; treating malformed input as an ordinary `none` outcome; checking canonical truth payload equality to select a branch; evaluating the subject twice; or running an unselected body.
- gaps: The bounded grammar constraint below is supplied, while E-4/E-11/E-24 retain the full concrete grammar, reference/admission traversal and malformed/nonliteral interfaces. E-18 retains exact branch payload-binding syntax; E-19 retains complete demand/order integration. E-3/E-12 retain declaration, lookup, dispatch, admission, construction and stopping charges. EV-098/112 and Rule E now transcribe the listed case/edge invoices as DRAFT; these are not adopted tariffs. Retain the reconsider-A open item below. Reconsideration item E-39; bounded grammar at CASE-GRAMMAR.md. R6: EV-086–113 now draft the listed K-13 operation table and E/L (former Rule S retired) [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json] after bounded G0172 review; this does not complete or adopt the whole meter. R10 drafts only the EV-122–125 structural/no-turn/entry/admission-boundary/unit proposal. R10 N10 supplies the conditional fresh completed-unary EV-024/026 witnesses; incomplete/warmed/resumed/restoration and general integration remain open. ## EV-041 — Primitive-result identities and payload premises

- revision: 3
- standing: DRAFT — builtin identity and primitive-versus-user payload core; current separately authenticated edges are referenced without adopting full grammar, bytes or resource contracts.
- kind: definition
- subject: DRAFT — an evaluator build under candidate builtin-plus-user local bindings, on admitted values
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:505–516 (EV-041, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:512–523 (EV-041); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json.
- text: DRAFT — Every program has the builtin complete choice identities `order/3`, `truth/2` and `option/2`; their names are reserved against user declarations. Retain the inherited ordinals: order less/equal/greater are 0/1/2, truth no/yes are 0/1, option none/some are 0/1. The name notation identifies the semantic names and is not a concrete byte encoding. Primitive signatures, produced values and case targets use these full identities. Each primitive-produced case in these families other than option.some has exactly empty `Row()` as its payload; a primitive-produced option.some has its one returned value as payload. Generic program constructors are not restricted to those primitive payload shapes: they may put any evaluator value into a builtin case. Thus generic truth number 1 holding `5` is a different value from canonical truth.yes holding `Row()`, although both match a branch for truth/2 ordinal 1. Complete external identities remain intact, so valid external truth/3 does not match local truth/2. This law supplies no new rule assigning a primitive or edge outcome to none/some.
- case: DRAFT — A supplied primitive-produced `Case((order,3),0,Row())` selects branch 0 of target order/3; valid external `Case((order,2),0,Row())` gives bare `none` and executes no branch against target order/3. A supplied primitive-produced `Case((option,2),1,5)` has one payload `5`. Generic `Case((truth,2),1,5)` is admitted by the candidate payload rule and differs in value from canonical `Case((truth,2),1,Row())`; both select branch 1 of a truth/2 case expression. A primitive result advertised as canonical truth.yes but carrying `5` violates the primitive-result rule. A user declaration truth/3 is refused, independent of admitting a valid externally supplied truth/3 value.
- excludes: DRAFT — Binding a primitive's truth result to truth/3 by its name; enforcing the primitive empty-row restriction on all generic builtin constructions; returning a nonempty primitive truth payload as canonical truth.yes; treating generic truth payload variation as a different choice; or applying local name reservation to rewrite foreign choice identities.
- gaps: The generic-versus-primitive payload distinction is drafted, with full recursive reference/admission traversal, canonical bytes, complete equations/interfaces and charges still E-4/E-11/E-14/E-16/E-18/E-3/E-12/E-27. EV-070–079/082–084 retain the surviving specified defaults; EV-080/081 are retired [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json]; the carrier law alone does not assign them. Case16 B is EV-038/040. R6: EV-086–113 now draft the listed K-13 operation table and E/L (former Rule S retired) [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json] after bounded G0172 review; this does not complete or adopt the whole meter. R10 drafts only the EV-122–125 structural/no-turn/entry/admission-boundary/unit proposal. Remaining natural and byte entries, positive-loop setup/turn A/R/T/counter charges, separate admission resources, complete meter integration, EV-018/021 witnesses, exact spellings and any host calibration outside semantic units remain open. ## EV-042 — Immutable set, member order and result families

- revision: 4
- standing: DRAFT — PROPOSED reviewed K-14 invariant/result core with all six extensional equations and delegated Q3 domain; full executable admission/interfaces/encoding/meter remain incomplete. INFERENCE — The current R16 revision awaits independent review; any inherited positive review covers only its original scope and bytes.
- kind: definition
- subject: an evaluator build on valid finite sets under the conditional K-3 canonical order/interner and canonical primitive-result premises
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:517–528 (EV-042, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:524–535 (EV-042); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json. R16 current source: OWN_WRITE; ONE_BRIEF:399–405,413–418; C_WRITE_REVIEW:64–82; R13:992,994,996,1004,1006 (under review). [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json]
- text: PROPOSED A set is an immutable finite collection of admitted evaluator values without repeats. It may contain sequence identities directly and at every finite immutable nesting depth through rows, cases and sets. Every admitted set supplied by a permitted literal, input, reachable starting-state value or construction satisfies the same invariant: sorted distinct members in K-3 order and one canonical set identity for each member collection. Sequence identities are atomic equality/order keys; current contents never enter comparison. Mutating a contained sequence changes neither the immutable member value nor its set's membership, order or identity. EV-049–054 supply empty/insert/remove/member/size/members over valid sets. Member/remove query any admitted value; an absent query gives canonical truth.no/the same set respectively. An admitted non-set first operand returns bare canonical none under the separately authenticated EV-084 edge; Q3 does not admit malformed values.
- case: PROPOSED Apply F. In both supplied states S4 has the single member Row(Case(chi,0,S1)), members(S4)=Row(w4), size(S4)=1, member(S4,w4)=truth.yes and member(S4,b)=truth.no. insert(S4,w4)=S4 and remove(S4,b)=S4 exactly. insert(S4,b)=U4 with members Row(w4,b); remove(U4,b)=S4 with that same canonical identity, while S4 and U4 themselves remain unchanged. These observations survive the contained a contents change. The admitted wrong-kind invocation size(0) gives bare none under EV-084; malformed admission remains distinct.
- excludes: PROPOSED Changing a set or member identity/order when a's contents change; substituting a contents snapshot for a handle; assigning duplicate insertion/absent removal a fresh observable set identity; mutating S4 during construction/removal; or treating a wrong-kind none result as admitting malformed values or as extending the valid-set equations to non-sets.
- gaps: The extensional equations and all-depth admitted-value domain remain DRAFT, with valid-member/query domain unchanged. EV-084 fixes wrong-kind non-set operands to none; malformed admission is not that default. E-4/E-14/E-16/E-18/E-27 retain complete traversal/canonical bytes/interfaces/bounds and metering. EV-063 supplies initial/live roots, not the executable visiting algorithm. ## EV-043 — Proposed strict reference evaluation order (K-5)

- revision: 3
- standing: draft — PROPOSED K-5 core; neither reviewer adopts it.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build executing the proposed reference evaluation
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:529–540 (EV-043, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:536–547 (EV-043); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json.
- text: Evaluate the arguments of a primitive or named call, row fields, a case's payload and set-operation operands once each in strict left-to-right order. Evaluate a let initializer before its body even if the bound value is unused. Evaluate a case subject and then only its selected branch. Evaluate a counted loop's bound and then its initial accumulator once each before its turns, then evaluate the body once per turn under the eventual loop-enumeration contract. If a required next step cannot be paid, stop there and retain the changes already made in the executed prefix. This does not determine the internal preflight/commit boundary of that next step.
- case: With `s : row(0)` and `i ∈ VALID(s)`, `row(WRITE(s,i,1),contents(s))` returns a row whose second field is `row(1)` under the candidate positional-write/contents contracts. A call `k(WRITE(s,i,7))` where `k` ignores its argument still changes `s` to hold 7. Likewise `let unused=WRITE(s,i,8); 0` changes `s` to hold 8. A case whose subject changes `s` to 2, whose selected branch changes it to 3 and whose unselected branch would change it to 4 has the subject/selected-branch changes only. For the selected loop bound two (counter0 then1), let bound, initializer and body each make distinguishable valid writes labelled B, I and T: the executed write order is B,I,T,T. If a later required event cannot be paid, the already executed write prefix remains; no later write occurs. SOURCE — Wherever this case assumes two turns for bound two, those turns are now selected (counter0 then1), not an eventual owner branch. [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-loop-o1-a-and-arch-fixes.json; E-22/O1 A, bound two only] PROPOSED — General positive equations and complete loop invoices remain open.
- excludes: The second field `row(0)` from reading the contents before the first row field; omission of an unused argument or let initializer; execution of an unselected branch; loop order I,B,T,T or repeated evaluation B,I,T,B,I,T; removing earlier executed writes solely because a later required event was unpayable. These are conditional semantic distinctions, with no claimed numeric meter witness.
- gaps: This K-5 order remains DRAFT: selecting strict sequence reads does not adopt every order rule. EV-078/082/083 retain initializer effects for empty make and zero/nonnatural loops; EV-040 now confines else to same-choice uncovered ordinals and runs no branch for foreign/noncase subjects. Earlier argument effects remain before ordinary none; resource stops preserve the committed prefix. Complete grammar/interfaces/order/debits remain E-4/E-18/E-19/E-27. ## EV-044 — Proposed once-only shared initializer (K-6)

- revision: 2
- standing: draft — PROPOSED valid-domain make core.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build executing valid-domain make
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:541–552 (EV-044, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:548–559 (EV-044); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json.
- text: Under the proposed strict argument order, `make(n,e)` evaluates its initializer `e` exactly once, including when the admitted length is zero. It then produces a fresh sequence with `n` positions, each holding that one realized initializer value. If that value is a sequence handle, every position holds the same handle; make does not independently realize an initializer for each position or copy that inner sequence's state into distinct sequences.
- case: `make(2,make(1,0))` realizes one inner sequence and one outer sequence. The outer contents row has two occurrences of the same inner handle. A permitted later write to that inner sequence is a change to that one shared inner sequence, reached by both outer fields. `make(0,make(1,0))` still realizes the inner sequence exactly once and produces an empty outer sequence: two allocations under the stated fresh-handle premise. Alternatively, `make(0,WRITE(s,i,9))`, with `i ∈ VALID(s)`, still performs the initializer write and returns an empty outer sequence.
- excludes: Three allocations and two distinct inner handles for the positive-length case from rerunning the inner make per position; only the outer allocation for the zero-length case; omission of the zero-length initializer write. No direct sequence-position read interface is used to state these distinctions.
- gaps: EV-065/078 supply strict fresh make/trail and the owner-decided zero-length rule. Preserve this detailed once-only/sharing witness. Exact signatures, interrupted make, complete identity/import bytes and safe result/temporary/work/trail charges remain E-18/E-23/E-25/E-27. ## EV-045 — Proposed valid write operation and result (K-6)

- revision: 3
- standing: draft — PROPOSED valid-domain positional-write operation, including trail placement and built-in result. R3_STAY's narrower identity-preserving change decision is adopted independently; it does not adopt all this operation's details.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build executing valid-domain sequence write
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:553–564 (EV-045, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:560–571 (EV-045); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json.
- text: With admitted sequence `s`, selector `i ∈ VALID(s)` and admitted realized replacement value `v`, the proposed `WRITE(s,i,v)` replaces the value held at that position of `s`. Every holder retains the same sequence and hence the same changed state. The completed write contributes its change to the ordered trail and returns the built-in affirmative `truth.yes` case under the separately drafted built-in carrier/payload premises. The owner decision supports mutation with the same sequence, while this exact primitive result and trail operation remain candidates.
- case: Let `a` and `b` denote the same `s : row(4)`, with `i=0` its valid selector under EV-061. A completed `WRITE(a,i,9)` leaves `a` and `b` denoting that same `s`, whose contents row is now `row(9)`, returns the proposed built-in affirmative truth case, and records the completed change. A later unpayable event retains that completed write prefix under EV-043.
- excludes: Preserving `s : row(4)` while returning only a distinct replacement sequence holding 9; splitting `a` and `b` into separate sequence identities; under the candidate result contract, returning the replacement sequence instead of the affirmative truth case; omitting the completed write solely because it was never subsequently observed.
- gaps: EV-068 supplements valid positional write with position-size charging and no marker. EV-076 independently decides outside-range none/no mutation/no trail. The successful truth.yes/result/trail core remains DRAFT, including same-value-write and commit details. Fixed length/no whole-write remain decided. The listed reservation/work/trail rows are now DRAFT; omitted formulas, full comparison events and broader protocol debt remain E-27. ## EV-046 — Proposed immutable contents row and live contained handles (K-9)

- revision: 3
- standing: draft — PROPOSED shallow row/contained-handle core.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build representing an admitted row or computing proposed contents
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:565–576 (EV-046, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:572–583 (EV-046); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json. R15 SOURCE — METHOD:70 and ARCH_REPAIRS:70–78 (F005). PROPOSED — Clause markings distinguish existing denotations/duties/notes only; no behavior is added or removed.
- text: [definition] A row is immutable in its immediate values. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build representing the stated admitted values, computing proposed contents or observing an admitted row] The proposed `contents(s)` returns the row of the values that `s`'s positions hold at that moment. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build representing the stated admitted values, computing proposed contents or observing an admitted row] A held sequence contributes its sequence handle, rather than a recursive capture of its contents. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build representing the stated admitted values, computing proposed contents or observing an admitted row] A later replacement in `s` does not replace a field of the previously returned row. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build representing the stated admitted values, computing proposed contents or observing an admitted row] A later mutation of an inner sequence does not replace its handle in that row, but the same handle continues to denote the changed inner sequence. [requirement; subject: an evaluator build representing the stated admitted values, computing proposed contents or observing an admitted row] An admitted case containing such a handle has the same distinction; a set containing such a handle has the same distinction under the decided Q3 all-depth member domain (EV-056). [requirement; subject: an evaluator build representing the stated admitted values, computing proposed contents or observing an admitted row] On an admitted immutable row, the valid-domain length observation is its number of immediate fields, and selection at a supplied valid row selector returns that immediate value. [definition] Positions start at zero under EV-061.
- note: [note; addressed to authors and reviewers] This field correspondence still chooses no operation spelling, direct sequence-read interface or malformed/outside-range behavior.
- case: Start with outer sequence `s` holding inner sequence `t : row(0)`. Obtain `r=contents(s)`, so `r` has the immediate field `t`. Replace the outer position with a distinct handle `u`; `r` still has field `t`. Then change the valid position of `t` from 0 to 1. `r` still has the same immediate field `t`, which now holds 1. A row containing an immediate natural 0 instead remains a row containing 0 after the outer sequence replaces that position. The one-field row r has length 1; selecting its supplied valid row field returns t both before and after those changes. This selects the handle, not a recursive copy of its current contents. For the two-field immutable Row(10,20), zero-origin field 1 is 20 under the eventual valid row-selection interface; the exact operation spelling remains E-18.
- excludes: Updating `r`'s field to `u` when the outer position changes; turning `r`'s field into a detached sequence frozen at 0; treating fixed identity of a contained handle as evidence that its reachable contents are fixed.
- gaps: EV-067 supplies completed nonempty contents and EV-079 the empty-row result; EV-080 is retired. EV-074 separately decides row/bytes outside-range bare none, without some wrapping. Preserve the shallow-field/live-handle witness; stable identity is not frozen history. Detached deep capture, exact interfaces/bytes, interrupted contents and complete costs remain E-18/E-26/E-27/E-35. ## EV-047 — Conservative transitive state-free eligibility under the strict read

- revision: 5
- standing: DRAFT — conservative transitive syntactic eligibility gate, updated to the decided strict read and the agreed separate-name principle; complete grammar, analysis and implementation remain open. INFERENCE — The current R16 revision awaits independent review; any inherited positive review covers only its original scope and bytes.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build deciding which first-order definitions may use memoization
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:577–588 (EV-047, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:584–594 and OLD_NAMES carry the safe gate and D's agreement in principle. C_R2:93–107 supplies its conservative-class interpretation and required deciding cases. ARCH:44–55,119 supplies the strict operation boundary. EDGES:145–160 records Arch's separate-name agreement and D-5 confirmation. NAMES_D5 /owner_line,/authorizes_only matches raw human SHA256 `945371d901870bb4e7b49766ea897eb0cb7f6e6bda2bc2185340a530cd175ae6`; DROP_STRICT matches raw human SHA256 `de13da5fc7b7af5ce80f01fc3df9dbeb2a0e88776032a6abfded6c022037a8e5`. R14 sources: owner-record-2026-11-11-web-oq1-oq3-a.json#OQ1, DIGEST.json#F006. R16 current source: OWN_WRITE; ONE_BRIEF:399–405,413–418; C_WRITE_REVIEW:64–82; R13:992,994,996,1004,1006 (under review). [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json]
- text: Only a definition proved state-free may use memoization. The conservative gate inspects its whole syntactic body and the bodies of all definitions in its finite transitive first-order call closure, including recursive components and bodies or branches that will not execute. Under the decided strict interface, every occurrence of `make`, `contents` or a positional sequence write is forbidden in that closure; every make is forbidden even at length zero or when its result is unused. `contents` reads mutable contents even if its returned value happens to be unchanged or discarded. Unknown or unresolved callees cannot pass. Primitive meanings must be fixed and calls resolved. An access whose binding is still ambiguous between immutable access and state access cannot pass; a proved immutable row/byte access may pass the access part, and a fixed immutable-only access with no fallback to sequence state does not need a generic parameter to have an immutable outer shape merely to avoid a state effect. The strict interface contains no positional sequence read; adding that later option would require the gate to exclude it as stateful. Carrying a stable sequence handle, including within immutable rows/cases/sets, or comparing stable identities does not itself read mutable contents. With fixed sequence lengths and a length observation that changes no sequence state, length may pass this state restriction; that permission does not pardon other operations in its helper's closure. The owner's condition for separate immutable and sequence names is met by Arch's and D's agreement in principle. Exact spellings and concrete grammar remain open, and an immutable operation must never fall back to sequence access on wrong shape. An invalid memo mark is refused before the run under EV-059 rather than silently ignored. This gate is exact only for its stated transitive syntactic effect class; it remains conservative for semantic state independence. SOURCE — Computing a term once and reusing its result is permitted only when the term makes nothing new, has no effect, has no open choice, consumes nothing use-once, and has a result that may be copied. State-freedom alone is insufficient. [decision: owner-record-2026-11-11-web-oq1-oq3-a.json; OQ1 A with Arch condition] Passing this syntactic state predicate alone grants no result-reuse licence. This adds no memo-mark admission/refusal outcome for an unproved freshness, choice, consumption or copyability premise; that proof and placement remain E-13/E-21.
- case: The following inline finite deciding families cover ambiguous access versus established immutable shape and fixed immutable-only access; nested stable identity; direct/helper/recursive-component `contents` or write; empty and positive fresh allocation; unknown callees; dead forbidden branches; fixed length versus a future hypothetical resizing interface; immutable wrong shape without state fallback; and a strict-only mutable-contents control. In particular `f(s)=contents(s)`, a helper closure reaching it, and a never-selected branch containing it are ineligible; `f()=make(0,7)` is ineligible. A closed immutable-row projection and stable-handle comparison pass this restriction when their entire closures pass. A valid fixed-length observation may pass, while a helper that also calls contents cannot. These are author-drafted deciding cases, not executed Loom/meter tests.
- excludes: Evading the check through helpers, recursive components or dead branches; caching fresh allocation merely because it is empty or unused; treating unknown calls or an unresolved overloaded access as state-free; allowing immutable-access fallback into sequence state; reusing stale mutable contents on an unsafe memo hit; rejecting handle carriage or comparison solely because a sequence occurs in immutable data; treating length as a blanket exemption for a stateful helper; or accepting an invalid memo mark and ignoring it. No workload prevalence estimate or complete semantic decision procedure is asserted.
- gaps: E-11/E-13 retain concrete grammar, mark syntax, complete call/outer-shape analysis, planned M-A several-argument keys (not current M-C rules) and incomplete-entry details; E-20 retains hit/call charge composition, and the listed K-13 table is now DRAFT after bounded G0172 review, with whole-meter debt retained. Exact separate operation spellings remain open although the principle is agreed. The generic overloaded-get limitation is historical or unresolved-binding context under the strict interface, not a measured claim that most row readers are refused. Future direct read and any future resizing interface require explicit follow-through; neither is installed here. Fresh versus warmed/resumed cache policy remains separate. Preserve EV-048's state-free boundary over the entire evaluated admission action, including result/literal/constructor-field/membership checks. Decided C8 outcomes do not turn stateful primitives into eligible ones. Exact naming/precision item E-40. R6: EV-086–113 now draft the listed K-13 operation table and E/L (former Rule S retired) [decision: owner-record-2026-11-12-write-charge-arch-and-one-brief.json] after bounded G0172 review; this does not complete or adopt the whole meter. R10 drafts only the EV-122–125 structural/no-turn/entry/admission-boundary/unit proposal. ## EV-048 — Proposed state-free boundary for the whole evaluated admission action (K-11)

- revision: 3
- standing: draft — PROPOSED restriction on D-ADM-5, with no stateful-run record readiness claim.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an admission procedure applying an evaluated symbol under D-ADM-5
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:589–600 (EV-048, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:596–607 (EV-048); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. Current updates: SOURCE CARD:40–47; ARCH:21–30,44–80,118–121; six authenticated owner records and OWNER_LINE_VERIFICATION.json. SOURCE R7_REVIEW:147: D-SEQ-3 (design R4:977 as quoted by G; original not reopened). R14 sources: DIGEST.json#F036, DIGEST.json#F026.
- text: The entire evaluated admission action must satisfy the proposed conservative state-free restriction. Its scope includes applying the definition and every invoked result check, literal check, constructor-field admission and membership check; checking only the named symbol's body is insufficient. All their called definitions are included by the transitive gate. The existing checked-value/use-once boundary remains, but passing this state restriction or proving only the evaluated body syntax-total does not establish the row or termination of the complete checked action. Its account must separately cover initial admission, build, body execution and every result/literal/constructor-field/membership check; it must distinguish an explicit rejection by a completed check from bounded-check exhaustion and from ordinary body results. A termination claim needs premises for every invoked check and transitive callee, the build and the resources they require, not merely body totality. Exact phase rows, outcome carriers, rejection placement and bounded-check exhaustion mapping remain OPEN in E-21; this rule grants no narrower completed-action row by omission. [digest: DIGEST.json; F036, F026] A stateful checker belongs to a separate state-bound run; it cannot inherit that admission promise from this rule.
- case: Let evaluated f be the state-free definition `f(x)=0`. Suppose checking the returned literal invokes `c`, which calls `contents(s)` for an available sequence handle. A body-only check of f would pass; the whole admission action does not pass the proposed gate. Replacing c with a membership check that performs a valid write similarly prevents this action from passing. If every invoked check and transitive callee is instead proved state-free and the ordinary admission conditions are met, this particular state restriction is satisfied. A stateful checker may be proposed separately, but its record/evidence is not established by this case. PROPOSED — Even if f(x)=0 is syntax-total, a completed result check may reject it, or a bounded result checker may exhaust before deciding. Those are separate outstanding whole-action outcomes, not proof of an ordinary accepted result. A total body with a nonterminating result checker has no complete-action totality conclusion. [digest: DIGEST.json; F036]
- excludes: Assigning a complete checked-action row from body totality alone, or allowing a hidden state read/write in result, literal or membership checking while inheriting the restricted row promise; treating a result label as evidence; allowing a stateful checker through solely because f's body is state-free; claiming that a stateful checker record is complete by citing unrepaired K-12.
- gaps: Keep the restriction over the entire evaluated admission action, including result/literal/constructor-field/membership checks; unknown invoked checks cannot pass. EV-064 now drafts the fresh-only record core. Rich stateful historical/scoped evidence, complete statement-form/theory/scope inference and breadcrumb/projection/restore duties remain E-21/E-26/E-35–37. Eval alone is not checker soundness. No complete meter or broader stateful admission alternative is installed. ## EV-049 — DRAFT set empty equation (K-14)

- standing: DRAFT — PROPOSED reviewed K-14 empty equation under the delegated Q3 member domain. INFERENCE — The current R16 revision awaits independent review; any inherited positive review covers only its original scope and bytes.
- kind: requirement

No set or extracted member value changes when a's contents change.
- excludes: PROPOSED Returning a nonempty empty result; assigning distinct observable empty-set identities by construction path; changing E or Sj through these operations; or using sequence contents to change membership, ordering or canonical identity.

## EV-052 — DRAFT set member equation (K-14)

- revision: 2
- standing: DRAFT — PROPOSED reviewed K-14 membership equation with canonical primitive truth results. INFERENCE — The current R16 revision awaits independent review; any inherited positive review covers only its original scope and bytes.
- kind: requirement
- subject: an evaluator build computing member(s,v) for valid set s and any admitted evaluator query value v
- from: SOURCE R6_RULES_BASE:637–648 (EV-052, pinned R5); inherited R5 citation scope follows: SOURCE OLD_RULES:644–655 (EV-052); earlier source/review/decision provenance and standing as recorded in this pinned R4 base, not reopened original files. - text: member(s,v) returns canonical truth.yes exactly when v belongs to M(s), and canonical truth.no otherwise. Its query domain is every admitted evaluator value, including direct and nested sequence identities. This observes fixed membership without changing the set or consulting a sequence's contents for comparison. A user-constructed truth case with another payload is not substituted for the canonical primitive result.
- case: PROPOSED Apply F in both Sigma0 and Sigma1. For each j=0–4, member(Sj,wj)=Case(truth/2,1,Row()), member(Sj,b)=Case(truth/2,0,Row()), member(Uj,wj)=truth.yes, member(Uj,b)=truth.yes and member(E,wj)=truth.no. For j=0, the absent b returns no even while a and b have equal contents. insert(Sj,wj)=Sj and remove(Sj,b)=Sj exactly, with members(Sj)=Row(wj) unchanged in both states. The comparisons do not alter either supplied sequence's state.
- excludes: PROPOSED Returning yes solely from equal sequence contents, returning no after a member's contents change, returning a natural or a noncanonical truth payload, changing sequence state through identity comparison, or mutating/reordering the set through membership testing.
- gaps: Preserve this DRAFT extensional equation and shared canonical set invariant for all incoming, literal and reachable-starting-state sets. The every-admitted-value all-depth member/query domain is decided; wrong-kind non-set operands return none under EV-084. ## EV-053 — DRAFT set size equation (K-14)

- revision: 2
- standing: DRAFT — PROPOSED reviewed K-14 cardinality equation. INFERENCE — The current R16 revision awaits independent review; any inherited positive review covers only its original scope and bytes.
- kind: requirement

- text: size(s) returns the natural cardinality of M(s), counting each distinct immediate member once. It leaves the canonical immutable set unchanged. Contained rows, cases, nested sets and sequence identities are values, not extra members to count by traversing their internals or sequence state.
